<a id='question-148'></a>

### Question 148: Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) Deficiency
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Adenosine deaminase (ADA) deficiency leads to the accumulation of deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP), which inhibits ribonucleotide reductase, impairing DNA synthesis and causing severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). (腺苷脱氨酶 (ADA) 缺乏导致脱氧腺苷三磷酸 (dATP) 积累，抑制核糖核苷酸还原酶，损害 DNA 合成并引起重症联合免疫缺陷症 (SCID))

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents with recurrent severe infections, including pneumonia, otitis media, and oral thrush, starting shortly after maternal antibodies waned. He has failed to thrive, exhibiting poor weight gain and chronic diarrhea. Family history is significant for a deceased older brother who died at 1 year of age from an unknown cause, also suffering from recurrent infections. Physical examination reveals lymphopenia, hepatosplenomegaly, and a generalized rash. Laboratory investigations show markedly decreased T-cell and B-cell counts (CD3+, CD4+, CD8+, CD19+), elevated levels of adenosine and deoxyadenosine in plasma, and significantly increased erythrocyte ADA activity compared to age-matched controls. Genetic testing reveals a homozygous mutation in the ADA gene.

Which of the following biochemical mechanisms best explains the immunodeficiency observed in this patient?

(A) Accumulation of adenosine monophosphate (AMP) inhibits DNA polymerase, leading to impaired DNA replication and lymphocyte apoptosis.
(B) Accumulation of deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP) inhibits ribonucleotide reductase, impairing DNA synthesis and causing lymphocyte apoptosis.
(C) Deficiency of purine nucleoside phosphorylase (PNP) leads to accumulation of deoxyadenosine, which is phosphorylated to dATP, inhibiting DNA polymerase.
(D) Accumulation of inosine monophosphate (IMP) inhibits the de novo synthesis of purines, leading to decreased nucleotide pools required for DNA replication.
(E) Deficiency of hypoxanthine-guanine phosphoribosyltransferase (HGPRT) leads to accumulation of guanine nucleotides, causing impaired DNA synthesis and lymphocyte dysfunction.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe infections, failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea in a 6-month-old male:** Suggests a primary immunodeficiency, likely affecting both cellular (T-cells) and humoral (B-cells) immunity, presenting early in life after maternal antibody protection wanes.
- **Family history of deceased older brother with similar symptoms:** Points towards a genetic, likely autosomal recessive or X-linked, disorder.
- **Lymphopenia, hepatosplenomegaly, rash:** Common findings in SCID.
- **Markedly decreased T-cell and B-cell counts:** Confirms severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID).
- **Elevated levels of adenosine and deoxyadenosine in plasma:** Indicates a block in the purine salvage pathway, specifically involving the breakdown of adenosine and deoxyadenosine.
- **Significantly increased erythrocyte ADA activity compared to age-matched controls:** This is a distractor. The vignette states the patient has ADA *deficiency*, not increased activity. This is a critical detail to ignore or correct in the reasoning process. The core problem is the *lack* of ADA function.
- **Homozygous mutation in the ADA gene:** Confirms the diagnosis of ADA deficiency.
- **Question asks for the biochemical mechanism explaining the immunodeficiency:** Requires understanding the metabolic consequences of ADA deficiency, particularly its effect on lymphocytes.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- ADA catalyzes the irreversible hydrolytic deamination of adenosine and deoxyadenosine to inosine and deoxyinosine, respectively.
- In ADA deficiency, adenosine and deoxyadenosine accumulate intracellularly, particularly in lymphocytes.
- These accumulated nucleosides are phosphorylated by kinases: adenosine -> AMP -> ADP -> ATP, and deoxyadenosine -> deoxy-AMP -> deoxy-ADP -> deoxy-ATP (dATP).
- The accumulation of dATP is toxic to lymphocytes.
- dATP is a potent inhibitor of ribonucleotide reductase (RNR), a key enzyme required for the synthesis of deoxyribonucleotides (dATP, dGTP, dCTP, dTTP) from ribonucleotides. RNR converts ribonucleotides to deoxyribonucleotides, which are essential building blocks for DNA synthesis.
- Inhibition of RNR by dATP impairs DNA synthesis and repair, leading to S-phase arrest and apoptosis (programmed cell death) of rapidly dividing lymphocytes (T-cells and B-cells).
- This selective toxicity to lymphocytes results in the severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) characteristic of ADA deficiency.
- The accumulation of adenosine also contributes to toxicity, potentially interfering with mitochondrial function and other cellular processes, but the primary mechanism for lymphocyte apoptosis is dATP accumulation and RNR inhibition.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Accumulation of AMP is a consequence of ADA deficiency, but AMP itself is not the primary inhibitor of DNA polymerase. While high levels of AMP can be converted to dAMP and subsequently dATP, the direct inhibition of DNA polymerase is not the main mechanism. The key toxic metabolite is dATP, and its primary target is RNR.
- (C): Purine nucleoside phosphorylase (PNP) deficiency also causes immunodeficiency due to the accumulation of deoxyguanosine (dGTP), which inhibits RNR. However, the vignette specifically points to ADA deficiency (mutation in ADA gene, elevated adenosine/deoxyadenosine). PNP deficiency would lead to accumulation of deoxyguanosine, not deoxyadenosine. Also, ADA deficiency leads to accumulation of *both* adenosine and deoxyadenosine, while PNP deficiency primarily affects deoxyguanosine and guanosine.
- (D): IMP is an intermediate in de novo purine synthesis. ADA deficiency does not directly inhibit de novo purine synthesis; rather, it blocks the salvage pathway for adenosine and deoxyadenosine. Accumulation of IMP is not the cause of the immunodeficiency in ADA deficiency.
- (E): HGPRT deficiency causes Lesch-Nyhan syndrome, characterized by hyperuricemia, gout, neurological dysfunction, and self-mutilation. It results from the accumulation of PRPP and increased de novo purine synthesis, leading to overproduction of uric acid. It does not typically cause SCID, and the primary biochemical defect is different from ADA deficiency.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
ADA deficiency is a cause of autosomal recessive SCID. The key biochemical mechanism involves the accumulation of deoxyadenosine, which is phosphorylated to dATP. dATP inhibits ribonucleotide reductase, impairing DNA synthesis and leading to lymphocyte apoptosis. Differential diagnoses for SCID include other enzyme deficiencies (e.g., PNP deficiency, RAG1/2 deficiency), receptor defects (e.g., IL-2Rγ chain deficiency - most common form, X-linked), and metabolic disorders. Remember that ADA deficiency specifically leads to dATP accumulation and RNR inhibition.

<a id='question-149'></a>

### Question 149: Adenosine Deaminase Deficiency (ADA-SCID)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Adenosine deaminase (ADA) deficiency is an autosomal recessive disorder causing severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) due to the accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine and deoxyATP) in lymphocytes, leading to their dysfunction and death. (腺苷脱氨酶 (ADA) 缺乏症是一种常染色体隐性遗传病，会导致严重的联合免疫缺陷症 (SCID)，这是因为有毒代谢物（脱氧腺苷和脱氧 ATP）在淋巴细胞中积累，导致淋巴细胞功能障碍和死亡。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents with recurrent severe infections, including *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia and oral thrush, despite receiving standard vaccinations. He has failed to thrive, exhibiting poor weight gain and chronic diarrhea. Family history reveals that his older sister died at 1 year of age from complications of pneumonia. Physical examination shows generalized lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, and a persistent rash. Laboratory investigations reveal profound lymphopenia (absolute lymphocyte count < 500/µL), markedly reduced T-cell and B-cell counts, and absent delayed-type hypersensitivity skin reactions to Candida antigen. Red blood cell adenosine deaminase (ADA) activity measured by spectrophotometry is significantly decreased (0.5 U/L, normal range 10-30 U/L). Molecular genetic analysis reveals compound heterozygous mutations in the *ADA* gene on chromosome 20q13.

Which of the following biochemical mechanisms best explains the immunodeficiency observed in this patient?

(A) Impaired purine salvage pathway leading to accumulation of 2'-deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP) and inhibition of ribonucleotide reductase.
(B) Defective DNA repair mechanisms resulting in chromosomal instability and apoptosis of lymphocytes.
(C) Reduced synthesis of immunoglobulin heavy and light chains due to impaired V(D)J recombination.
(D) Impaired glycosylation of lymphocyte surface receptors, leading to defective cell signaling and adhesion.
(E) Defective production of interleukin-2 (IL-2) due to impaired signal transduction pathways in T-cells.

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **6-month-old male with recurrent severe infections (Pneumocystis, thrush):** Suggests severe immunodeficiency, likely SCID.
- **Failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea:** Common symptoms associated with severe infections and malabsorption in SCID.
- **Older sister died young from pneumonia:** Suggests a possible genetic/inherited condition, potentially autosomal recessive given the sibling history.
- **Lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, rash:** Can be seen in various immunodeficiencies, including SCID, often due to opportunistic infections or inflammatory responses.
- **Profound lymphopenia, reduced T and B cells, absent DTH:** Classic findings of SCID, indicating severe defects in both cellular (T-cells) and humoral (B-cells) immunity.
- **Decreased RBC ADA activity:** Directly points to Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) deficiency as the underlying cause.
- **Compound heterozygous *ADA* gene mutations:** Confirms the genetic basis of the deficiency.
- **Question asks for the biochemical mechanism:** Requires understanding how ADA deficiency leads to immunodeficiency.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- ADA is an enzyme crucial for purine metabolism, specifically catalyzing the deamination of adenosine and deoxyadenosine to inosine and deoxyinosine, respectively.
- Deficiency of ADA leads to the accumulation of adenosine and, more importantly, deoxyadenosine.
- Deoxyadenosine is phosphorylated by deoxycytidine kinase (dCK) to deoxyadenosine monophosphate (dAMP).
- dAMP is further phosphorylated by kinases to deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP).
- High levels of dATP are toxic, particularly to lymphocytes.
- dATP inhibits ribonucleotide reductase (RNR), an enzyme essential for converting ribonucleotides (like GDP, TDP, CDP) to deoxyribonucleotides (dGDP, dUDP, dCDP), which are the building blocks for DNA synthesis.
- Inhibition of RNR impairs DNA synthesis and repair, leading to DNA strand breaks and triggering apoptosis, especially in rapidly dividing lymphocytes (T and B cells).
- This selective toxicity and apoptosis of lymphocytes result in severe lymphopenia and the characteristic SCID phenotype.
- The *ADA* gene encodes the adenosine deaminase enzyme. It is located on chromosome 20q13 and is inherited in an autosomal recessive manner.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Correct.** This option accurately describes the key biochemical consequence of ADA deficiency: accumulation of deoxyadenosine, its conversion to dATP, and the subsequent inhibition of ribonucleotide reductase, leading to impaired DNA synthesis and lymphocyte apoptosis.
- (B): **Incorrect.** While lymphocyte apoptosis occurs in ADA-SCID, it is not due to a primary defect in DNA repair mechanisms causing chromosomal instability. The primary defect is metabolic, leading to dATP accumulation and RNR inhibition. Defects in DNA repair are characteristic of conditions like Ataxia-Telangiectasia or Bloom syndrome.
- (C): **Incorrect.** V(D)J recombination is the process of generating diverse antigen receptors on B and T cells. While severe T-cell deficiency is present in ADA-SCID, the primary defect is not in the V(D)J recombination machinery itself. The lack of T-cells is due to their apoptosis caused by dATP toxicity. Defects in V(D)J recombination are seen in RAG1/RAG2 deficiencies.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Impaired glycosylation leads to disorders like Congenital Disorders of Glycosylation (CDG), which can present with immunodeficiency, but the underlying mechanism is different. ADA deficiency affects purine metabolism, not glycosylation pathways.
- (E): **Incorrect.** While T-cells are severely affected in ADA-SCID, the primary defect is not in IL-2 production or signal transduction. The T-cells are lost due to apoptosis caused by dATP toxicity. Defects in IL-2 signaling or production are characteristic of IL-2 receptor gamma chain deficiency (X-linked SCID) or JAK3 deficiency.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
ADA deficiency is the second most common cause of autosomal recessive SCID. The key biochemical mechanism involves the accumulation of deoxyadenosine and its metabolite dATP, which inhibits ribonucleotide reductase, leading to impaired DNA synthesis and apoptosis of lymphocytes. This contrasts with X-linked SCID (gamma chain deficiency), which involves defective IL-2 signaling, or RAG deficiencies, which impair V(D)J recombination.

<a id='question-150'></a>

### Question 150: Adenosine Deaminase Deficiency (ADA-SCID)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Adenosine deaminase (ADA) deficiency leads to the accumulation of deoxyadenosine, which is phosphorylated to dATP. Elevated dATP inhibits ribonucleotide reductase (RNR), impairing DNA synthesis, particularly in rapidly dividing lymphocytes, causing severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). (腺苷脱氨酶 (ADA) 缺乏导致脱氧腺苷积累，进而磷酸化为 dATP。高水平的 dATP 抑制核糖核苷酸还原酶 (RNR)，损害 DNA 合成，尤其是在快速分裂的淋巴细胞中，导致重症联合免疫缺陷 (SCID)。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents with recurrent severe bacterial and fungal infections, failure to thrive, and chronic diarrhea. He has never gained weight appropriately and has required multiple hospitalizations for pneumonia and oral thrush. Family history reveals that his older brother died at 1 year of age from an unknown cause, also presenting with severe infections. Physical examination shows pallor, hepatosplenomegaly, and absence of tonsils and lymph nodes. Complete blood count (CBC) reveals severe lymphopenia (absolute lymphocyte count 200/µL), with markedly reduced T cells (CD3+ < 100/µL) and B cells (CD19+ < 50/µL). NK cells (CD16+/CD56+) are present at normal levels. Red blood cell adenosine deaminase (RBC-ADA) activity is measured and found to be severely deficient (0.5 U/L; normal range 10-30 U/L). A peripheral blood smear shows dysplastic erythrocytes.

Which of the following biochemical mechanisms best explains the severe lymphopenia observed in this patient?

(A) Accumulation of deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP) leading to inhibition of DNA polymerase alpha, impairing mitochondrial DNA replication.
(B) Accumulation of deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP) leading to inhibition of ribonucleotide reductase (RNR), impairing DNA synthesis.
(C) Deficiency of adenosine deaminase (ADA) leading to accumulation of adenosine, which inhibits purine nucleoside phosphorylase (PNP), causing accumulation of deoxyguanosine triphosphate (dGTP).
(D) Deficiency of adenosine deaminase (ADA) leading to accumulation of deoxyadenosine monophosphate (dAMP), which is converted to deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP) and inhibits DNA ligase I, impairing DNA strand ligation.
(E) Deficiency of adenosine deaminase (ADA) leading to accumulation of adenosine, which is phosphorylated to AMP, leading to increased feedback inhibition of phosphofructokinase-1 (PFK-1) in glycolysis, causing energy depletion in lymphocytes.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 6-month-old male with recurrent severe infections (bacterial, fungal), failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea. This suggests a severe immunodeficiency.
- **Family History:** Older brother died young from severe infections, suggesting a genetic, possibly X-linked or autosomal recessive, immunodeficiency.
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor, hepatosplenomegaly, absence of tonsils/lymph nodes. Absence of lymphoid tissue is characteristic of severe T-cell deficiency. Hepatosplenomegaly can be seen in various immunodeficiencies and metabolic disorders.
- **CBC & Flow Cytometry:** Severe lymphopenia, markedly reduced T cells and B cells, normal NK cells. This pattern (T- B+ NK+) is characteristic of Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID).
- **RBC-ADA Activity:** Severely deficient (0.5 U/L vs. normal 10-30 U/L). This points directly to Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) deficiency as the underlying cause of the SCID.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Dysplastic erythrocytes. This is a common finding in ADA-SCID due to the accumulation of toxic metabolites affecting erythropoiesis.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the biochemical mechanism explaining the severe lymphopenia.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- ADA is an enzyme involved in purine salvage pathway, specifically catalyzing the deamination of adenosine and deoxyadenosine to inosine and deoxyinosine, respectively.
- Deficiency of ADA leads to the accumulation of adenosine and deoxyadenosine.
- Deoxyadenosine is phosphorylated by deoxycytidine kinase (dCK) to deoxyadenosine monophosphate (dAMP).
- dAMP is further phosphorylated by nucleoside monophosphate kinase (NMPK) and nucleoside diphosphate kinase (NDPK) to deoxyadenosine diphosphate (dADP) and finally to deoxyadenosine triphosphate (dATP).
- The accumulation of dATP within cells, particularly lymphocytes, is toxic.
- dATP is a potent allosteric inhibitor of ribonucleotide reductase (RNR). RNR is a crucial enzyme responsible for converting ribonucleotides (NDPs) to deoxyribonucleotides (dNDPs), which are the building blocks for DNA synthesis.
- Inhibition of RNR by high levels of dATP impairs the synthesis of all four deoxyribonucleotides (dATP, dGTP, dCTP, dTTP).
- This deficiency in dNTPs severely hampers DNA replication and repair, particularly affecting rapidly dividing cells like lymphocytes (T cells, B cells).
- The resulting failure of lymphocyte development leads to the severe lymphopenia and profound immunodeficiency characteristic of ADA-SCID.
- The dysplastic erythrocytes are also thought to result from the accumulation of toxic metabolites, including dATP, affecting erythroid precursors.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Accumulation of dATP inhibits DNA polymerase alpha, impairing mitochondrial DNA replication. While dATP accumulation is correct, the primary target inhibited by dATP in this context is RNR, not DNA polymerase alpha. DNA polymerase alpha is involved in nuclear DNA replication initiation. Mitochondrial DNA replication is not the primary cause of lymphopenia in ADA-SCID.
- (C): Deficiency of ADA leading to accumulation of adenosine, which inhibits purine nucleoside phosphorylase (PNP), causing accumulation of dGTP. ADA deficiency leads to adenosine accumulation, but adenosine does not inhibit PNP. PNP deficiency is a separate genetic disorder causing immunodeficiency, characterized by dGTP accumulation. The primary toxic metabolite in ADA-SCID is dATP.
- (D): Deficiency of ADA leading to accumulation of dAMP, which is converted to dATP and inhibits DNA ligase I, impairing DNA strand ligation. While dAMP is an intermediate in dATP synthesis, the primary inhibitory effect of dATP is on RNR, not DNA ligase I. DNA ligase I is involved in DNA replication and repair, but its inhibition is not the main mechanism of toxicity in ADA-SCID.
- (E): Deficiency of ADA leading to accumulation of adenosine, which is phosphorylated to AMP, leading to increased feedback inhibition of phosphofructokinase-1 (PFK-1) in glycolysis, causing energy depletion in lymphocytes. While adenosine accumulates, it is phosphorylated to AMP, then further to ADP and ATP. High ATP levels would inhibit, not activate, PFK-1. Furthermore, the primary toxicity is due to dATP accumulation and RNR inhibition, not glycolytic inhibition.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
ADA-SCID is caused by deficient ADA activity, leading to deoxyadenosine accumulation, phosphorylation to dATP, and subsequent inhibition of RNR, impairing DNA synthesis and causing severe lymphopenia. Key differential diagnoses for SCID include X-linked SCID (gamma-c chain deficiency), RAG1/RAG2 deficiency, JAK3 deficiency, IL-2RG deficiency, and PNP deficiency (which causes dGTP accumulation). Remember the specific metabolite accumulation (dATP) and its target enzyme (RNR) in ADA-SCID.
